{"forms":[{"form":"affine geometries","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"As an alternative to the axiomatic approach, affine geometry can be studied via the properties of affine transformations, which do not, in general, preserve distances or angles, but do preserve alignment of points and parallelism of lines.","type":"example"},{"text":"The notion of parallelism remains central to affine geometry, in which the parallel postulate is replaced by Playfair's axiom, a version of the postulate that relies on neither distance nor angle size.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["The branch of geometry dealing with what can be deduced in Euclidean geometry when the notions of line length and angle size are ignored."],"tags":["uncountable"],"topics":["geometry","mathematics","sciences"]},{"glosses":["A geometry that is otherwise Euclidean but disregards lengths and angle sizes."],"tags":["countable"]}],"upstream_line":442084,"upstream_offset":2091455269,"upstream_record_sha256":"96f592f7d61d42cb7e8c98cf0f1b771d7027a0f3a05b505a4a3b823ee6587e26","word":"affine geometry"}

{"etymology_number":"1","forms":[{"form":"glands","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["A specialized cell, group of cells, or organ of endothelial origin in the human or animal body that synthesizes a chemical substance, such as hormones or breast milk, and releases it, often into the bloodstream (endocrine gland) or into cavities inside the body or its outer surface (exocrine gland)."],"topics":["biology","natural-sciences","zoology"]},{"glosses":["A specialized cell, group of cells, or organ of endothelial origin in the human or animal body that synthesizes a chemical substance, such as hormones or breast milk, and releases it, often into the bloodstream (endocrine gland) or into cavities inside the body or its outer surface (exocrine gland).","A structure resembling a gland, especially a lymph node."],"topics":["anatomy","biology","medicine","natural-sciences","sciences","zoology"]},{"glosses":["A secretory structure on the surface of an organ."],"topics":["biology","botany","natural-sciences"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The gland of a tap/faucet is usually packed with string which has been soaked in tallow or similar grease.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A stuffing box used to seal a rotating or reciprocating shaft against a fluid."],"topics":["engineering","natural-sciences","physical-sciences"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/ɡlænd/","tags":["Canada","General-American","Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"[ɡleənd] ~ [ɡlɛənd]"},{"ipa":"/ɡland/","tags":["British","Northern-England","Scotland","Southern","Standard","Wales"]},{"ipa":"/ɡlæːnd/","tags":["Australia"]},{"ipa":"[ɡleːnd] ~ [ɡlɛeːnd]"},{"ipa":"/ɡlɛnd/","tags":["New-Zealand"]}],"synonyms":[{"word":"gland"}],"upstream_line":178125,"upstream_offset":846036348,"upstream_record_sha256":"9c51ba45df7e3cdde1ba68c9b587d4e4c541d686a9c173d20ac2f75544ac5e95","word":"gland"}
{"etymology_number":"2","forms":[{"form":"glands","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["A compressable cylindrical case and its contents around a shaft where it passes through a barrier, intended to prevent the passage of a fluid past the barrier, used for example around a ship’s propeller shaft or around a tap, valve or faucet."],"topics":["engineering","mechanical-engineering","mechanics","natural-sciences","physical-sciences"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/ɡlænd/","tags":["Canada","General-American","Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"[ɡleənd] ~ [ɡlɛənd]"},{"ipa":"/ɡland/","tags":["British","Northern-England","Scotland","Southern","Standard","Wales"]},{"ipa":"/ɡlæːnd/","tags":["Australia"]},{"ipa":"[ɡleːnd] ~ [ɡlɛeːnd]"},{"ipa":"/ɡlɛnd/","tags":["New-Zealand"]}],"synonyms":[{"word":"gland"}],"upstream_line":178126,"upstream_offset":846068767,"upstream_record_sha256":"f6ce4916efce1dc1ffb08e987f0be775dc08b4d02b8a374f49324f5811970980","word":"gland"}
